## 3. Phase 4 — Adviser Review & Feedback

Phase 4 Adviser Review & Feedback remains complete. Phase 7 is the latest accepted milestone; see the final closure handoff.

### Verified workflow

```text
Student submits
      ↓
Adviser notified
      ↓
Adviser starts review
      ↓
Under Review
      ↓
Revision Required ──→ Student resubmits a new version
      │
      └──────────────→ Approved
```

Established Phase 4 behavior:
- Adviser access is scoped to their own research groups.
- Advisers can start review.
- Advisers can provide feedback and select `Revision Required` or `Approved`.
- Submission/research status updates are coordinated.
- Submission versions are preserved.
- Student/adviser in-app notifications are triggered.
- Adviser decisions are activity-logged.
- Cross-adviser isolation was verified.
- The end-to-end review/resubmission/approval workflow was verified.

Relevant commits:
- `c425f1a` — `feat(submission): enforce unique paper versions`
- `cad078c` — `feat(notification): notify students of adviser decisions`
- `4499aea` — `feat(notification): notify adviser of new submissions`
- `bc87caf` — `fix(review): close Phase 4 regression audit`

Notification delivery failure handling was deferred within the Phase 4 closure audit scope. Phase 5.2 separately verified post-commit notification failure as non-blocking.

## 4. Phase 5 Status

Phase 5.1 Document Storage Foundation and Phase 5.2 Submission ↔ Document Integration are complete and runtime-accepted.

The stable design in `docs/SOURCE_OF_TRUTH_UPDATED.md` calls for:
- private Supabase Storage
- bucket `research-submissions`
- PDF-only documents
- maximum file size of 10 MB
- storage path pattern `research/{paperId}/{version}/{sanitizedFileName}`
- server-side authorization before document access
- server-generated signed URLs
- 300-second / five-minute signed URL expiry
- database storage of the storage path rather than a public URL

### Phase 5.2 implementation and acceptance

- Submission version calculation and eligibility checks are serialized per paper using a transaction-scoped PostgreSQL advisory lock; the unique `(paper_id, version)` constraint remains the database backstop.
- If a concurrent request loses, it observes the committed state and is rejected without leaving an uploaded object.
- Adviser feedback, conditional submission status, research status, and activity log writes use one database transaction. Competing decisions cannot both commit.
- Student/Adviser notifications are attempted after the primary operation commits. Runtime fault injection confirmed notification failure does not make submission/review appear failed or roll back primary records.
- Runtime acceptance was service-level, not browser-level. Synthetic fixtures were cleaned up. Phase 5.2 required no schema or migration changes.
- Migration `0009_repository_paper_unique.sql` was not applied during Phase 5.2; it was subsequently applied only to the designated development/test database during Phase 5.4.

### Phase 5.3 — Secure Document Access — Complete

Submitted-document access requires an authenticated Student/Adviser session, positive submission ID validation, current active-account/database-role verification, Student group membership or Adviser group ownership, persisted submission/file metadata verification, and a storage path bound to the submission paper/version. Admin and Panel are denied under the submitted-document rules. Only the persisted path is passed to the existing server-only signer for 300 seconds in private bucket `research-submissions`; no client-supplied paper, path, version, identity, or role is trusted.

```text
Authenticated request
→ submission ID validation
→ active-account/database-role verification
→ Student membership or Adviser ownership authorization
→ persisted submission/file metadata verification
→ storage path bound to submission paper/version
→ server-side 300-second signed URL
```

All accepted live scenarios and independently verified fixture cleanup passed; see the live acceptance handoff below. No Phase 5.3 schema/migration changes were required or applied.

Already issued signed URLs may remain usable until their 300-second expiry after authorization is revoked. Legacy/nonconforming paths outside the established naming convention are intentionally rejected; do not weaken validation. Full submission/review regression evidence is service-level; browser acceptance specifically covered document access. These are known limitations, not blockers.

### Phase 5.4 — Repository Publication — Complete and runtime accepted

Phase 5.4 — Repository Publication is complete and runtime-accepted (PASS; completion/documentation audit complete). Phase 5.5 — Repository Document Access is complete and runtime-accepted (PASS). Phase 5.6 — End-to-End Verification & Security Audit and Phase 5.7 — Documentation & Completion Audit are complete. **Phase 5 overall is complete.** Phase 6 — Search, Filtering & Discovery is next and requires explicit owner direction; it was not started in this pass. CI remains deferred. Do not commit or push.

## 6. Stable Project Rules

These rules remain applicable:

- Layered architecture: Pages/UI → Server Actions → Zod validation → Services → Repositories → Drizzle ORM → PostgreSQL.
- Business logic and authorization belong in services.
- Repositories remain focused on data access.
- Authorization must be enforced server-side.
- Verify ownership/group access before protected operations.
- Students do not self-join research groups; advisers assign/remove students.
- A student has one active group membership.
- Preserve submission versions rather than overwriting them.
- Submission versions use unique `(paper_id, version)`.
- Notifications are currently in-app only.
- Notification failure must not block the primary operation.
- Panel Evaluation is excluded/deferred.
- AI assistance must not replace final human academic/review decisions.
- Supabase secret credentials remain server-only.
- Store storage paths rather than public document URLs.
- Generate signed document URLs server-side.
- Keep test and production environments/data separate.
- Never commit secrets.

## 7. Database / Migration Safety

`docs/DATABASE (1).md` is the database reference, while the actual `db/schema/`, `db/migrations/`, and target database are authoritative.

The documentation historically referred to 11 core tables while explicitly naming 10. **Do not invent an eleventh table.** Verify the actual schema before resolving that discrepancy.

Migration `0009_repository_paper_unique.sql` was applied in Phase 5.4 only to the designated development database after confirming no duplicate paper IDs, exact schema compatibility, and the migration ledger. Other environments remain unverified.

Before applying schema changes:

```bash
npx drizzle-kit check
```

Then review the generated SQL and target data before migration.

## 10A. Merged Phase 8 Update - 2026-10-09

Source: supplied Master Context and Source of Truth v2 documents. Reported decisions are merged here; prior test evidence is retained, not independently rerun.

### Phase 8 — New Approved Owner Decisions (2026-10-09)

The older generative AI Chatbot Guide and AI Research Summarization are
**retired**. Approved replacement:

| Audience | Feature | Provider |
|---|---|---|
| Admin (verified eligible adult) | Interpret approved non-identifying aggregate administrative report statistics | Groq API Free tier, **disabled** |
| Adviser (verified eligible adult) | Explain authorized aggregate submission/review workload and procedural workflow guidance | Groq API Free tier, **disabled** |
| Student | Predefined clickable questions and canonical static answers; no free text or external AI requests | None |
| Panel | Excluded | None |

This merge records the supplied scope-change decision as documentation only. No application implementation, provider activation, manuscript editing or commit is performed.

### Security/Privacy Gate (Unresolved; Do Not Enable Groq)

- Server must verify session, active account, role, scope, and **adult
  eligibility independently**; no assumption that Admin/Adviser implies 18+.
- Trusted server-side aggregation may query authorized database metrics;
  **Groq/provider modules cannot directly** query PostgreSQL, access
  Supabase Storage, PDFs, research content, signed URLs, or arbitrary web.
- Send only approved, minimized, non-identifying aggregate statistics.
  No names, titles, abstracts, feedback text, credentials, or raw records.
- Suppress potentially identifying small-count aggregates. The numeric
  threshold and eligible metric catalog remain design decisions.
- Strict server-side request contracts, role separation, prompt-injection
  resistance, rate/concurrency limits, safe output handling, fallback and
  operational logging without sensitive payloads are mandatory.
- Provider access requires deployment egress controls where available;
  omitting model tools is not itself a network firewall.
- **Gating approvals outstanding:** Groq's applicable age/use terms,
  privacy/data processing, free-tier quota/limits and cost strategy,
  adult-verification design, deployment/security controls, and owner
  activation approval. No AI service should run before approval.
- AI must not grade, approve/reject, produce official Adviser feedback,
  or replace human research judgment.

### Proposed Phase 8 Continuation (Design Only)

This numbering is a **working design sequence**, not evidence of completed
work and not authorization to implement:

1. **8.1 revised architecture/scope design:** Reconcile retired proposal
   and define the three separate feature contracts.
2. **8.2 static foundation:** Preserve previously implemented guide;
   verify Student remains entirely provider-independent.
3. **8.3 Admin report interpretation design:** Approve metric allowlist,
   provider-safe aggregation, privacy thresholds, interpretive limits,
   output/fallback, and existing report-page integration.
4. **8.4 Adviser workflow assistant design:** Scope metrics strictly to
   own authorized groups, prohibit official feedback, and design safe
   procedural guidance/fallback.
5. **8.5 Groq integration/security (only once approved):** Feature flag,
   server gateway, adult eligibility, limits, privacy and network controls.
6. **8.6 focused acceptance/closure:** Roles, privacy, small counts,
   injection, failures, browser behavior, lint, TypeScript and build.

Avoid introducing schema/migration/dependency changes without specific
technical need and explicit authorization. Use existing Admin reporting
metrics where feasible; do not invent statistics or assume they exist.

Published repository access is a separate existing flow and remains outside this change.
- Implementation: The action authenticates/role-gates, validates a positive PostgreSQL submission ID with Zod without coercion, and calls a server-only document service. The service rechecks the current active account and database role, resolves submission → paper → group, checks Student membership or Adviser ownership, and validates that the persisted PDF path exactly matches the stored paper and version before signing for 300 seconds. Missing metadata and DB/storage errors return null. Signing errors no longer log raw SDK errors. Student PDF navigation uses the current tab, matching the existing Adviser button.

Inactive/deleted accounts, other groups/Advisers, Admin, and Panel are denied by the submitted-document flow. Admin denial preserves the existing action's rules; Admin published-repository access is unchanged. No client paper ID, path, version, identity, or role is used for signing.
- Files changed: `lib/actions/storage.action.ts`, `lib/services/submission-document.service.ts`, `lib/validations/submission-document.ts`, `lib/services/storage.service.ts`, `app/dashboard/student/submissions/DownloadSubmissionButton.tsx`, `tests/phase5-document-access.unit.cjs`, `tests/phase5-document-access.acceptance.cjs`, `AGENTS.md`, and `docs/MASTER_CONTEXT_UPDATED.md`.

## Handoff - 2026-10-09 - Phase 8.5B.4A AI Eligibility and Consent Backend Foundation

- Status: BACKEND FOUNDATION implemented and checks PASS; NOT OPERATIONAL until separately approved migration application, PostgreSQL acceptance and independent verifier bootstrap. No UI or live provider integration.
- Branch / starting and ending HEAD: feature/development at ca95fd3 (feat(admin): complete Phase 7 reporting). Existing Phase 8/owner Source of Truth changes preserved; all work unstaged/uncommitted, no commit/push.
- Schema decision: Approved manual server configuration replaces the proposed designation table. Two append-only domain event tables are sufficient: ai_eligibility_events captures subject, decision/role/account-management revision, certifying verifier and configured authorization reference (or external bootstrap reference), timestamp and revoked flag; ai_consent_events captures subject, eligibility event binding, terms version/digest, accepted/declined/withdrawn and timestamp. Missing eligibility means Pending, missing consent means denied; no automatic expiry, birthdate, document upload or users/research table change.
- Verifier configuration: lib/auth/ai-verifier-config.ts reads only AI_INSTITUTIONAL_VERIFIERS_JSON server-side: JSON array of {userId,authorizationRef}; malformed/missing/duplicate/extra-field configuration denies all. Maximum 32 entries, positive integer IDs and bounded opaque authorization references. No configuration set in .env or deployment now; no returned/logged allowlist, verifier-management dashboard or web bootstrap endpoint. Certification requires configured active database Admin AND independently Eligible verifier; self-certification/reset/revocation denied.
- Backend: Strict actions delegate to Zod/services/repository. User rows locked in deterministic ID order; decision and any existing consent withdrawal append in one transaction. Expected revisions reject stale/concurrent/replayed mutations. Explicit Eligible/Not Eligible/Pending and revoked=true events preserved; no history update/delete API. Role/account-management updatedAt binding invalidates approval after account changes, including role-away-and-back/reactivation. Re-certification requires fresh consent. Existing auth/user/research workflows untouched.
- Consent: Server-owned ai-assistance-v1 text/digest; explicit literal acceptance=true and current version required, client identities/digests/timestamps rejected. Accept requires independently Eligible active Admin/Adviser. Decline/withdraw preserve ordinary access; withdrawal also works after eligibility or role loss for the active account. Latest event must be accepted with current version/digest and current eligibility-event ID. Material changes require a new immutable version and renewed acceptance; text needs institutional review before a future UI/live use.
- Authorization: Existing ai-authorization boundary adds getAIInterpretationAuthorization, delegating to locked active-account/current-role -> independent eligibility -> current consent checks. Returns internal revisions only. getAIProviderAccount always denies while Groq is hard-disabled, even with valid entitlement/flags. Existing local aggregation remains explicitly local, not a provider authorization gate; future optional interpretation must use this entitlement boundary before ownership/privacy/quotas and recheck revisions before delivery. Existing static actions short-circuit without registry queries; removed the outdated claim that all adults are unverified. Legacy no-account eligibility probe remains intentionally deny-only for compatibility and is not the persisted entitlement API.

### Minimal real-PostgreSQL acceptance procedure (planned; not executed)

1. Owner approves the exact target, a restorable backup and migration-only application. Immediately recheck ledger through 0009 and absence of 0010 objects; use the reviewed Drizzle migration path, not push or manual SQL plus fabricated ledger entries. After application independently verify 0010 hash/timestamp, two tables, five FKs, CHECKs/indexes and unchanged existing constraints. Migration approval alone does not authorize bootstrap or fixture writes.
2. Separately authorize synthetic fixture writes/cleanup, preferably in a disposable migrated local test database. Use a uniquely marked test Admin verifier, another Admin, an Adviser and Student/Panel/inactive fixtures; no real research, storage or documents. Test-only institutional bootstrap references are clearly synthetic and must never confer operational approval on real accounts. Any real tester/verifier approval instead requires actual independent institutional verification. Keep Groq disabled and all credentials/payloads out of logs.
3. Use BEGIN/savepoints/ROLLBACK for direct CHECK/FK rejection cases: invalid decisions/kinds, NULL certification evidence, self-certification, nonexistent references, accepted consent without eligibility, invalid digest and invalid revocation combinations. Verify history reference deletion is rejected. Rollback does not reset sequences; disposable test database preferred. Confirm the known same-subject FK limitation separately; ensure application cannot exploit it.
4. Exercise actual services/repository against PostgreSQL with synthetic authenticated sessions: missing configuration, unapproved/inactive/stale-role verifier, self-approval and Student/Panel certification denial; missing eligibility means Pending; Eligible/Not Eligible/reset/revocation persist as successive events without overwriting. Current consent acceptance requires explicit acceptance/current terms/current eligibility; missing/declined/withdrawn/outdated consent denies entitlement. Revocation/recertification atomically appends withdrawal, and fresh acceptance binds the new event. Role/deactivation/reactivation or updated_at change invalidates eligibility. Verify timestamp round-trip under the driver/session time zone, including sub-millisecond timestamps.
5. Run two real database connections against the same expected revision: one mutation succeeds, the stale one denies; verify deterministic account locks, no partial event/consent writes and rollback on injected failure. Read back after reconnect to prove persistence. Entitlement may succeed for eligible/consenting Admin/Adviser, but provider account always remains null; assert zero outbound attempts. Static Student guide, ordinary login/reports/review access remain available without eligibility/consent. Clean only explicitly approved synthetic fixtures in FK-safe order, or discard the disposable database; never delete existing history to simplify tests.

### Manual verifier bootstrap (future approval required; not performed)

1. Institution designates an independent officer and authorized DB operator. Officer independently verifies the initial active Admin adult account and issues an approved opaque audit reference outside the application; no birthdate, ID documents or self-attestation stored. Approve the subject and bootstrap separately from migration application.
2. Operator uses a parameterized, locked transaction on the approved target: SELECT that users row FOR UPDATE; verify role_id=1, is_active=true and no eligibility history. Insert exactly one ai_eligibility_events row using user_id from the locked account, decision='Eligible', event_kind='bootstrap', decision_role_id=1, account_updated_at copied directly from users.updated_at, external_authority_ref from institutional approval, revoked=false and verifier_user_id/verifier_authorization_ref=NULL; let id/recorded_at default. Require exactly one insertion, verify fields, then commit. Abort on existing history; recovery is a separate audited decision with consent invalidation, never an overwrite/replayed bootstrap.
3. Configure AI_INSTITUTIONAL_VERIFIERS_JSON only in secure server-side operator configuration: a strict array of entries with the approved positive userId and institution-issued opaque authorizationRef (1-80 alphanumeric/underscore/hyphen characters), no duplicates/extra fields, at most 32. Do not expose values to clients, logs, repository or NEXT_PUBLIC variables. Restart all relevant processes; independently verify config denial/approval behavior without displaying the list. No verifier dashboard or application designation API exists. Removal from configuration prevents future certifications; existing issued approvals require separate audited revocation/remediation rather than assuming removal revokes them automatically.
4. Verifier cannot approve their own eligibility or consent for another account. Institutionally approved, versioned consent must be explicitly accepted by each eligible user; bootstrap/configuration does not create consent. Review consent text before future UI or provider use. All live Groq/local-pilot/Vercel approvals remain separate.

- Status: Documentation/review COMPLETE; no provider approval inferred from this task. feature/development at ca95fd3, existing work preserved/unstaged/uncommitted. ### Verified official findings (checked 2026-10-09)

- The [Services Agreement](https://console.groq.com/docs/legal/services-agreement), modified June 22, 2026, requires age 18+, institutional contracting authority when acting for the institution, lawful inputs/notices and output evaluation. Inputs/outputs cannot be used to train/fine-tune models without explicit customer permission/instruction. Model-specific terms also apply. A college capstone is not exempt; this is not a legal compliance certification.
- Prior missing-Service-Specific-Terms blocker RESOLVED for document availability: the [legal index](https://console.groq.com/docs/legal) redirects its link to [Terms of Use](https://groq.com/terms-of-use), but the bottom contains a separate Service-Specific Terms section, effective November 13, 2025. Its published addition covers designated beta-model Kimi processing under the DPA, not the selected production GPT-OSS model. The website-only disclaimer above does not negate that separate section. Institution still reviews applicable terms; no special unpublished agreement presumed.
- [Your Data](https://console.groq.com/docs/your-data): inference content not retained by default; reliability/abuse logs can remain up to 30 days or longer if legally required. Usage metadata is retained. ZDR is publicly available to all customers and disables reliability/abuse content retention; actual organization setting not inspected. Retained customer content is in US GCP buckets. No absolute zero-retention/anonymity/local-residency guarantee. [DPA](https://console.groq.com/docs/legal/customer-data-processing-addendum): customer controller/processor responsibilities, subprocessors and international processing (including US) remain institution decisions. [Privacy Policy](https://groq.com/privacy-policy) addresses account/website/usage information as controller; it explicitly separates customer API data governed by Agreement/DPA. Do not conflate website policy with inference terms.
- Recommendation: pin text-in/text-out production model openai/gpt-oss-20b; it is listed in the [catalog](https://console.groq.com/docs/models) and supports [strict JSON-schema outputs](https://console.groq.com/docs/structured-outputs). Its optional tool/search/code capabilities are excluded from this feature: use plain chat-completions, no tools/compound/agent endpoints, and reject tool output. Schema conformance is not factual or academic safety assurance; preserve Zod/reference/content checks and fallback. [Free limits](https://console.groq.com/docs/rate-limits): public summary 30 RPM, 1,000 RPD, 8,000 TPM, 200,000 TPD, organization-wide; actual console exceptions/model access not verified. No automatic model switch.
- [Billing](https://console.groq.com/docs/billing-faqs): Developer upgrade needs a payment method and introduces usage charges. Keep the account Free; no paid upgrade/fallback/quota bypass. Public Free availability is not evidence of the owner's actual plan/payment settings. [Acceptable Use](https://console.groq.com/docs/legal/ai-policy) prohibits quota circumvention via multiple accounts/organizations.

### Consent review and concise replacement proposal (not installed or approved)

Current v2-draft already states optional use, aggregate minimization, inaccurate outputs/academic limits, withdrawal, proposed limits and pending approval. Missing before real requests: concise provider retention/training/US-processing disclosures, official policy links, actual organization settings and institution contact. Keep draft preparation acceptance distinct from approval for live use; publish a new immutable version/digest with renewed unchecked acceptance after owner/institution approval. Do not edit accepted v2 text in place.

Proposed user notice:

> AI assistance is optional for institutionally verified adult Admins and Advisers. During this local pilot, only fictional, reviewed aggregate bands and fixed instructions go to Groq. Names, research content, documents, feedback and eligibility/consent records are excluded. AI may be wrong; it cannot grade, approve submissions, make Adviser decisions or produce official feedback. Groq retains usage metadata; inference content is not retained by default, but reliability/abuse logs may remain up to 30 days or longer when legally required. ZDR configuration must be confirmed. Retained content is stored in the US. Model training requires explicit customer permission, which this pilot will not grant. You may decline or withdraw without losing normal features or static guidance; withdrawal stops future requests but cannot recall sent data. Pilot limits: 3 attempts per account/day, 10 total/day, 120-second cooldown, one request at a time. See the linked Services Agreement, DPA, Your Data and Privacy Policy. Institutional contact and privacy approval: pending. Groq remains disabled until separately authorized.

This is proposed plain-language disclosure, not a finalized legal agreement. When moving beyond synthetic data, explicitly replace the fictional-data scope and reapprove purpose/privacy/consent. Record institution eligibility/consent audit retention and contact simply; do not invent a retention deadline or add a dashboard.

## Handoff - 2026-10-14 - Phase 9.5 Minimal Online-First PWA Integration

- Status: **Implemented - awaiting owner review.** Owner authorized Phase 9.5 only. Verified `feature/development` at `59a4d60` with a clean tree; Phases 9.1-9.4 are committed. This supersedes the preceding NOT STARTED statement, preserving earlier evidence. No closure or commit claimed.
- Framework approach: read installed Next.js 16.2.9 manifest, application-icon, metadata, viewport and PWA guides under `node_modules/next/dist/docs/`. Built-in `app/manifest.ts` automatically associates `/manifest.webmanifest`; no duplicate manual manifest link. Theme/color scheme use the supported `viewport` export, not deprecated metadata fields. The framework's `appleWebApp.capable` generates `mobile-web-app-capable` in this version, with Apple title/status-bar metadata and an explicit Apple touch icon. No PWA package, service worker or push example adopted.
- Manifest: name `ThesiSHS AI`, short name `ThesiSHS`, description `Senior High School research management system.`, start URL `/dashboard`, scope `/`, standalone display, theme/background `#f6f8fb`. Root scope keeps existing login and dashboard routes in scope; scope is navigation configuration, never authorization. Public manifest/icons contain no account or research information. Static caching of these public assets is distinct from private-content/offline caching.
- Assets: `public/icons/thesishs-192.png` (192x192, 2,035 bytes), `public/icons/thesishs-512.png` (512x512, 5,815 bytes), `public/icons/thesishs-apple-180.png` (180x180, 1,850 bytes). Generated with installed Windows System.Drawing tooling, no dependency or external image service. Temporary blue TS monogram, existing light background and plain bold lettering, not a permanent logo. Replaced starter `app/favicon.ico` with matching 32x32 TS favicon (377 bytes). PNG dimensions verified from served PNG headers and assets visually inspected; actual PNG MIME and HTTP 200 responses verified. Apple icon also checked at 180x180.
- Metadata/UI: `app/layout.tsx` retains Phase 9.1 title/description and adds application name, Apple standalone/title/default status-bar settings and Apple icon; viewport theme is light with `colorScheme: light`. `app/dashboard/page.tsx` adds one small labeled installation-help section with native details/summary. Android Chrome, iPhone Safari and desktop Chrome/Edge guidance accounts for menu/version differences; installation is optional and network remains necessary. No aggressive popup, install event tracking, new client component, second login or settings module. Four scoped CSS rules in `app/globals.css` bound reading width, spacing and the 44px native summary target, reusing existing panels/focus/type/colors.
- Guidance sources checked: [Chrome Android](https://support.google.com/chrome/answer/9658361?hl=en&co=GENIE.Platform%3DAndroid), [Chrome desktop](https://support.google.com/chrome/answer/9658361?hl=en&co=GENIE.Platform%3DDesktop), [iPhone Safari](https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/open-as-web-app-iphea86e5236/ios), [Edge](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/edge/install-manage-or-uninstall-apps-in-microsoft-edge). Browsers offer different installation/menu behavior; production HTTPS and real-platform acceptance remain separate. No browser capability universally guaranteed by this implementation.

## Handoff - 2026-10-15 - Phase 9.7.3 Shared Light, Dark and System Themes

- Status: **Implemented — awaiting owner visual review.** Focused implementation/automated acceptance PASS. Phase 9 remains open; Phase 9.7.4 Shared Settings Page is the next planned checkpoint, subject to separate owner approval. No Settings route, notification dropdown or later checkpoint implemented.
- Baseline: feature/development at e04fb6c (feat(phase9): add role-aware collapsible dashboard navigation), staging empty. Exactly five original pending Phase 9.6 paths: docs/MASTER_CONTEXT_UPDATED.md; tests/phase9-auth-dashboards.browser.cjs; tests/phase9-pwa.browser.cjs; tests/phase9-submission-review.browser.cjs; tests/phase9-final-readonly.acceptance.cjs (untracked). No material discrepancy. Final inventory: 15 modified and six untracked paths, all unstaged, same HEAD. This task touches 17 paths; the four original pending tests are untouched and this document is append-only.
- Architecture: one root ThemeProvider with React useSyncExternalStore, one browser preference and one reusable native ThemeSelector. Stable server snapshot renders a disabled System selector until hydration; client snapshot/subscription reads browser APIs only on the client. No theme library or added dependency. Components can be reused by a future approved Settings screen.
- Behavior: light/dark explicitly select appearance; system resolves prefers-color-scheme and tracks live browser/device changes. Missing/invalid stored preferences resolve System. Browser localStorage key thesishs-theme stores only light, dark or system when selected; no default write. Preference is per device/browser, independent of account and retained across client navigation, refresh, login pages and logout. Cross-tab storage changes synchronize selection; clearing storage returns System. Blocked storage falls back to System on page load; selection still works in memory and survives client navigation, but cannot survive a full reload without persistence.
- First paint: fixed, input-free early inline head script validates the stored preference and sets only html data-theme before visible content. Follows installed Next.js 16.2.9 preventing-flash-before-hydration guide. Root-only suppressHydrationWarning accommodates this deliberate attribute change; server content/auth rendering remains intact. Browser-native color-scheme follows effective root theme; viewport metadata allows light dark. Pre-hydration Chromium probe blocked React scripts and confirmed persisted Light wins over a Dark browser preference with the expected light body background. VM checks cover all preference/system combinations and unavailable storage. No hydration errors observed.
- Security approach: no application CSP was configured in inspected sources; no CSP/header/security permission changed. Script contains no user data, arbitrary stored content or executable interpolation. A future strict deployment CSP must authorize this exact script via its approved hash/nonce mechanism; this checkpoint does not weaken CSP. Browser chrome themeColor and installed PWA assets remain unchanged; native controls/scrollbars use the effective CSS color-scheme.
- Controls: landing placeholder replaced by explicit Appearance select (Light/Dark/System), compact in the existing header; identical control above Login/Register forms and at the top of the shared authenticated shell, including existing Panel pages and pages without DashboardHeader. Existing navigation, active states, collapse, drawer and logout behavior preserved. No custom menu, focus trap, added animation or decorative icon.
- Tokens: global semantic page/surface/secondary/hover/text/muted/border/input/action/focus/error/notice/brand/sidebar/status tokens retain original light values. Restrained dark slate/navy surfaces, lighter navy action colors, readable pale text and muted teal/amber statuses replace their equivalents. Both CSS modules consume the same root variables. Shared forms, file inputs, tables, statistics, notifications, repository details and governance dialogs inherit the palette; the sole hardcoded Adviser group border now uses the shared border token. Status text and existing icons remain meaningful without color. No business logic or service contract changed.

This is the current consolidated Phase 9.7.5 report, including all owner-requested refinements. It supersedes earlier approval-only title emphasis, below-Settings collapse placement and left-aligned collapse-icon descriptions. Earlier handoffs and test evidence remain preserved as history.

- Notification architecture: existing notifications model, repository, service and delivery events reused; full-history listing remains unchanged. Bounded recipient-scoped preview and unread-count queries added without schema/migrations. No duplicate notification system, new delivery event or fabricated destination.
- Shared bell: one compact 44px Notifications bell alongside the existing theme control; accessible name, expanded state, full-count announcement and visible keyboard focus. Available to active current-role Student, Adviser, Admin and Panel accounts.
- Unread behavior: actual authorized count; zero has no badge, 1-9 show the number, above nine shows 9+. Unknown/error counts are not reported as zero. Opening does not mark records read. Count refresh follows confirmed successful mutation.
- Recent preview: five newest own notifications ordered created_at DESC, id DESC; bold action titles, three-line message previews, explicit read/unread state and timestamps. All action headings are bold (font-weight 700), including approval/revision/review titles when supplied by existing events, in both dropdown and full history. No new status/event generation introduced.
- Popover interaction: toggle, Escape, outside click/focus and navigation dismissal; predictable focus restoration and post-read focus recovery. Scrollable viewport-contained panel, sticky history footer, 44px actions and no decorative animation.
- Hidden scrollbar: chrome hidden while overflow-y:auto retains native mouse-wheel, touch-swipe and keyboard scrolling. No wheel/touch/key scroll prevention or custom scrolling engine.
- Read actions: individual Mark as Read retains its existing action signature and ownership service. Added explicit Mark all as read for every own unread notification, including records beyond the five-item preview. Server derives recipient from session/active account; one user-scoped unread-only UPDATE. Pending labels/disable, shared mutation lock, duplicate-click protection and failure feedback; zero/unknown/loading states disable bulk read. Failed mutation retains last confirmed count; successful mutation triggers fresh data and existing history/dashboard revalidation.
- History routes: /dashboard/student/notifications, /dashboard/adviser/notifications and /dashboard/admin/notifications. Static role-safe links remain available even during fetch errors. Panel has no history link per explicit owner choice to preserve existing routes; its bell shows actual authorized previews/empty state. Earlier below-Settings placement is superseded. Mobile menu/drawer behavior retained.
- Loading/empty/error: compact Loading notifications; You're all caught up; concise fetch/read failures with Retry. Failed fetch clears unknown preview/count without blocking header rendering; history navigation remains accessible.

No client user ID or arbitrary database URLs trusted. DTO omits recipient IDs. Bulk operation cannot change another recipient's records.

- Shared header: removed only the redundant content branding link; sidebar branding retained. Title and existing bell/theme controls share the first grid row; welcome text spans the row beneath. Narrow-screen title sizing prevents mid-word breaks. Existing titles, role navigation, theme persistence and combined header architecture retained; no Adviser/Admin content redesign.
- Dashboard: content-sized Research Group and Latest Feedback cards sit side by side on desktop and stack group-first on mobile. No workspace shortcut or management forms. No group produces one contextual panel. Actual group metadata, member names/count and inline View/Hide Members control replace decorative content; members start collapsed with aria-expanded/aria-controls.

Active/current-role checks precede reads; group/paper IDs come only from authorized group projects. Client DTO contains no storage paths, credentials, submitter IDs or teacher IDs. Existing submitter-only feedback visibility is preserved: team versions may be listed under an authorized group project, but another submitter's feedback is not exposed.
- Latest status: per group, actual submissions across its authorized projects are ordered submittedAt DESC, id DESC. Associated project title/version is shown; newest-created project is not assumed to contain the latest submission. Badge uses persisted submission status; no-project/not-submitted states are descriptive text, not new statuses.
- Latest feedback: most recent authorized review by createdAt DESC, feedback id DESC; three-line visual excerpt, version/date and View Full Feedback. Validated project/submission/feedback parameters select the correct project, expand the exact version, and subtly highlight/scroll/focus the stable feedback ID. Missing, repeated, malformed, oversized, foreign-project and mismatched targets fall back without exposing records. No new feedback route or schema.
- Research Submissions: compact selector defaults to createdAt DESC, id DESC. Local switching changes details, status, upload association, history and reviews together from the same authorized DTO, without asynchronous selection races. Modal-open switching is disabled. Project title/category/status stay visible; View/Hide Details reveals full abstract and keywords inline.
- Dialogs: existing creation fields/Zod/service and PDF action/validation reused. Creation action now returns safe result feedback plus confirmed project ID and revalidates the actual submissions route. Successful creation navigates to that project while retaining the success notice. Native modal dialogs add explicit first/last Tab looping, return focus, Escape/backdrop/Close dismissal, unsaved-change confirmation, pending dismissal protection, synchronous duplicate-request lock, beforeunload protection, and scrollable mobile layout. Native onSubmit retains file/remarks/fields on errors. Upload has a fixed hidden stable paperId for the selected project, preserves PDF/10 MiB/remarks/version/storage rules, and refreshes after confirmed success.
- History: only selected-project versions, numeric version DESC with timestamp/ID tie-breakers. Newest is initially expanded unless an authorized target overrides it; one version or none can be open. Manual collapse survives unrelated UI updates. Project changes reset expansion appropriately. Remarks, permitted feedback and existing combined View / Download PDF component/action retained; no public/permanent URLs. New loading/error boundaries provide safe status and retry. Student timestamps explicitly use en-PH/Asia/Manila to avoid server/client timezone hydration differences.

- B1 authorization: getUsers previously performed an unrestricted repository read; role/status actions trusted a session role, while their services accepted a caller-supplied currentUserId. getUsers, changeUserRole and setUserStatus now independently derive identity from the authenticated session using the existing getActiveAIAccount helper and require a current, existing, active database Admin. Actions delegate to these protected services and return controlled denials without depending on page navigation.
- Mutation persistence: new updateManagedUserForAdmin repository transaction locks actor and target in ascending ID order, rechecks current active Admin authority and self-change denial, then updates only the intended role or status and existing updatedAt revision. Direct repository recheck also denies inactive/former Admin actors. Existing four-role changes and activation semantics remain; no group membership or account lifecycle rules were added or removed. Existing activity names/descriptions remain. A confirmed transaction with a failed activity log returns confirmed account success with an activity-recording warning. Unconfirmed write outcomes return a non-sensitive refresh/verify message and uncertain flag; the UI pauses further submissions. No blind retry or false optimistic update.
- Account creation: existing session/current-Admin checks, transaction-local actor lock/recheck, Admin/Adviser-only schema, password policy/confirmation, hash and email handling remain. Authorized validation messages are surfaced, and uncertain write results are handled explicitly. The existing ID-only successful result contract is retained; no credentials are returned and no eligibility event is created automatically.

AdminAccountCreation reuses the unchanged shared ResearchDialog for native modal focus entry/containment/restoration, Escape/backdrop/Cancel/Close controls, dirty-form confirmation and beforeunload protection, synchronous duplicate locking, pending-disabled fields/dismissal and short-screen scrolling. All six existing fields remain. One parent modal state closes creation before mounting eligibility with the exact returned account ID. - Concurrency limits: a revocation committed before the mutation obtains the actor lock is observed and denies the write. If the mutation holds that lock first, it may complete before a later role/status change; real cross-connection tests verify both orderings. This is transaction ordering, not instant cancellation of an already-authorized transaction. Read authorization and listing remain separate reads rather than a transaction-consistent snapshot.
- Mutation results: group success, validation failure, denial, missing/invalid target and uncertain write outcomes are distinguishable. - Concurrency limits: final FOR UPDATE checks use existing PostgreSQL transaction ordering. A role/activity change or deletion that obtains the account lock first is observed and denied; a protected write that obtains the lock while authorized may commit before a competing revocation. This is not instant cancellation or permanent authorization through future work. No actor lock spans upload I/O; revocation during upload can leave a transient object until cleanup. Existing group/membership/assignment races and the known unconditional start-review status race remain outside scope, as do connection-pool behavior and upload-transaction reliability.

## F2-B1 - Phase 7 Reporting Runner Connection Cleanup - 2026-10-19

Deadline aborts HTTP requests/body consumption; finally always aborts remaining HTTP work and awaits the existing client's end({ timeout: 5 }). Original failure type/call sites are reported before cleanup; secondary cleanup failure is separately labelled and never replaces it. Cleanup-only failure sets nonzero status. A six-second cleanup watchdog is referenced while shutdown is pending, then unreferenced so normal completion exits naturally; it can exit only this runner if shutdown/owned handles stall. ### Separate owner-authorized React 310 investigation - no application fix implemented

Correction to the initial failure attribution: the auth runner's accumulated error list already contained React 310 before clicking the loaded Adviser Dashboard's own Dashboard link. The event trace locates the first error during an Admin visit to /dashboard/adviser, redirected through /dashboard to /dashboard/admin. A second error occurred for Admin -> /dashboard/student. The Adviser self-link itself passed isolated direct tests; it must not be described as the proven trigger.

Observed matrix: correct-Adviser direct load, browser refresh, Settings -> Dashboard and self-link PASS without React 310 on the reused production build at 1280/360 where sampled; the owner's development server also completed direct/refresh/Settings/self-link checks without React 310. Development Adviser Groups -> Dashboard additionally passed a GET-only control. Development cross-role controls completed without React 310. The dev console did emit HMR WebSocket ERR_INVALID_HTTP_RESPONSE handshake failures; this is a separate environment limitation and was not hidden as a hook exception.

Confirmed production defect: Admin -> Adviser streamed authorization redirect produces React 310 even when every POST/non-read browser request is aborted. Thus a completed notification Server Action is not necessary. GET response inspection in both dev and production found HTTP 200 with __next-page-redirect meta refresh to /dashboard for unauthorized Adviser/Student entry; Adviser fallback had already streamed. /dashboard then redirects to the account's own destination. The existing service role check is inside AdviserOverview under explicit Suspense; Student authorization is inside the route's loading boundary.

Captured production stack: updateWorkInProgressHook (minified aP) -> useMemo -> Next Router (minified L in static chunk 3t1w8zn0ilonz.js, column 217338), then React rendering frames. CDP captured the actual component/fiber ancestor stack L -> y -> h -> s -> R -> I -> F -> context/strict/root tags without props, identity or research content. The failing Router already had three current/alternate hook records and failed when requesting the subsequent memo hook. Installed source identifies L as Router in next/dist/client/components/app-router.js. React 310 decodes to 'Rendered more hooks than during the previous render' (https://react.dev/errors/310). No conditional application Hook call or remount defect was established in AdviserPage, DashboardShell/Navigation, NotificationProvider/Bell or ThemeProvider/Selector. The confirmed boundary is Next's root Router during a late streamed authorization redirect. Exact upstream renderer/router internals and whether a dependency patch is required remain unproven; local observation does not justify a dependency upgrade or application Hooks rewrite.

Smallest corrective proposal, NOT implemented: evaluate the Adviser role guard before returning its Suspense subtree (app/dashboard/adviser/page.tsx), retaining service-level checks. Account for the same Student loading-boundary case with an early role segment boundary (likely app/dashboard/student/layout.tsx if required). Verify redirects resolve before a protected loading subtree starts; do not weaken authorization or suppress browser errors. Avoid changing the shared notification transport merely on the discredited action-completion hypothesis. Acceptance for any approved fix: production and dev anonymous/all six cross-role redirects with zero React errors, correct-Adviser direct/refresh/self-link/Groups navigation at 360/1280, preserved loading behavior, stale/inactive-account boundaries and notification preview behavior. The proposed guard adjustment is an unvalidated candidate until separately authorized implementation and regression. Expected benefit: avoid the observed late redirect path; risk: changed streaming/loading timing and possibly redundant auth reads. No source/library/dependency fix has been made.

An automatic approval review rejected the proposed delayed-POST control because it could continue mutation-capable server actions. That command did not execute. The investigation used the safer control aborting all POST/non-read methods, which still reproduced 310. No additional mutation permission was needed or used.

- [ ] Adviser signs in with a real password, opens the owned group's v1 submission, starts review (Under Review), enters recognizable synthetic feedback and requests revision. Confirm v1 and paper show Revision Required, and the Student can read that v1 feedback.

- [ ] Adviser starts reviewing v2 and approves it. Confirm v2 and the paper are Approved, while v1 retains its earlier decision/feedback. View/download the authorized v2 PDF and verify the v2 marker; open v1 once to confirm the original marker remains.
- [ ] Admin signs in with a real password, opens Repository management -> Ready to Publish, and publishes the approved paper once. Confirm Published/Repository Entries. Expect one published repository entry linked to the approved paper; underlying PDFs remain private and are served through authorized temporary links.

Basic notification visibility may be checked separately; exact internal notification/audit IDs are unnecessary.
